海德描述了一条实验验证路径，用来检验他对大气湍流中扭转时空光束的模拟。傅里叶变换脉冲整形器能够合成光束的单次实现。将许多彼此独立的实现按非相干方式相加，便可体现部分相干性。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C006_31 -->

<!-- WORD:W007 -->
# compromise

**词源**：compromise 源于拉丁语 compromissum，与“共同承诺、同意服从裁断”有关；com- 表共同，后半关联 promise。由共同让步记住和解义；现代“损害、危及”是另一语义分支，不能因为同一个词就把妥协都理解成有损原则。

<!-- USE:U007_01 -->
## compromise /ˈkɑmprəmaɪz/ n. · 双方互让达成的和解
`争执双方通过各自作出让步而达成的解决或这种处理方式，可数或不可数；常说 reach a compromise、a policy of compromise。concession 是一方的让步，give and take 强调相互迁就，trade-off 强调为一项收益牺牲另一项。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C007_01 USE:U007_01 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"双方互让达成的和解","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/cache/epub/27439/pg27439-images.html"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S007_01 -->
An attorney arrived with a client claiming damages to a wagon and with eyewitness testimony about the accident.
<!-- SENTENCE:S007_01_2 USE:U007_01 -->
Farrell settled on the spot with a cash compromise.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C007_01 -->
一名律师带着委托人前来，要求赔偿货车在事故中所受的损失，并提供了目击证言。法雷尔当场以一笔现金和解了结此事。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C007_01 -->
<!-- USE:U007_02 -->
## compromise /ˈkɑmprəmaɪz/ n. · 相互冲突需求间的折中
`不同观点、行动或现实需求之间的中间方案，常用 a compromise between A and B；不一定先有正式争执，也不保证各方面都最优。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C007_02 USE:U007_02 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"相互冲突需求间的折中","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/cache/epub/53551/pg53551-images.html"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S007_02 -->
Sarita wore sober black, with white cuffs and collar, and her hair was arranged with severe plainness.
<!-- SENTENCE:S007_02_2 USE:U007_02 -->
The costume seemed a compromise between that of a companion and a senior maid.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C007_02 -->
萨丽塔穿着素黑衣服，配白色袖口和领子，头发梳得极为朴素。那套装束看起来介于陪伴女伴与高级女仆的服饰之间，是两者的折中。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C007_02 -->
<!-- USE:U007_03 -->
## compromise /ˈkɑmprəmaɪz/ v. · 作出妥协互让
`通常不及物，双方以互让解决争执；compromise with somebody over/on something，或 compromise on 后接接受的条件。concede 偏承认或让步，meet halfway 突出双方各让一些，trade off 强调拿一项条件换取另一项。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C007_03 USE:U007_03 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"作出妥协互让","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/cache/epub/27439/pg27439-images.html"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S007_03 USE:U007_03 -->
The street-railway company preferred to compromise immediately rather than face the costs and trouble of a trial.
<!-- SENTENCE:S007_03_2 -->
The settlement money was placed with a special administrator for the heirs.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C007_03 -->
街道铁路公司宁愿立即妥协和解，也不愿承担审判的费用与麻烦。和解款被交给一名特别管理人保管，以便转交合法继承人。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C007_03 -->
<!-- USE:U007_04 -->
## compromise /ˈkɑmprəmaɪz/ v. · 以互让解决争执
`较旧的及物用法，直接接争执或分歧，表示通过双方让步解决；现代法律正式语境中仍可用 compromise a claim 表示通过和解处理一项请求。日常一般叙述常用 reach a compromise 或不及物 compromise on/over，不能把这种直接及物用法套用于所有对象。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C007_04 USE:U007_04 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"以互让解决争执","sourceUrls":["https://www.gov.uk/government/publications/charities-and-litigation-a-guide-for-trustees-cc38/charities-and-litigation-a-guide-for-trustees"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S007_04 USE:U007_04 -->
In its guidance for charity trustees, the Charity Commission for England and Wales says the duty to protect a charity’s assets applies equally when compromising a claim or bringing or defending legal action.
<!-- SENTENCE:S007_04_2 -->
Trustees should seek as favourable a settlement as reasonably possible for the charity.
<!-- SENTENCE:S007_04_3 -->
The guidance also calls for qualified specialist advice and a check that trustees have the power to carry out the settlement.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C007_04 -->
英格兰和威尔士慈善委员会在给慈善机构受托人的指南中指出，无论是通过和解处理请求，还是提起或应对诉讼，受托人保护机构资产的义务都同样适用。受托人应在合理可能的范围内争取对慈善机构尽量有利的和解。指南还要求咨询合格的专业人士，并核实受托人是否有权实施和解条款。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C007_04 -->
<!-- USE:U007_05 -->
## compromise /ˈkɑmprəmaɪz/ v. · 损害名誉或原则
`及物，因失当行为损害自己名誉、诚信或原则，常带不赞成；compromise oneself 与 compromise one’s principles 均可，不能与中性的协商折中混为一谈。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C007_05 USE:U007_05 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"损害名誉或原则","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/cache/epub/67403/pg67403-images.html"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S007_05 USE:U007_05 -->
Speer quotes Morley’s criticism of people who compromise their real opinions for gain, and of others who avoid free thought to preserve their emotional comfort.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C007_05 -->
斯皮尔援引莫利的批评：有些人为获利而牺牲自己的真实观点，另一些人则为了保持情感上的安适而回避自由思考。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C007_05 -->
<!-- USE:U007_06 -->
## compromise /ˈkɑmprəmaɪz/ v. · 危及安全或效能
`及物，使某事陷入危险或受损，可说 compromise safety/security；不一定牵涉名誉，也不表示双方达成协议。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C007_06 USE:U007_06 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"危及安全或效能","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/cache/epub/98/pg98-images.html"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S007_06 USE:U007_06 -->
As noon arrived without the Doctor’s return, every minute of delay threatened to compromise Tellson’s bank.
<!-- SENTENCE:S007_06_2 -->
Mr. Lorry therefore consulted Lucie about finding separate lodgings nearby.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C007_06 -->
到了中午，医生仍未回来，每多耽搁一分钟都有危及台尔森银行的可能。因此，洛里先生与露西商量，打算在附近另找住处。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C007_06 -->
<!-- USE:U007_07 -->
## compromiser /ˈkɑmprəmaɪzɚ/ n. · 愿意协调妥协的人
`可数，作妥协或促成折中的人；是否褒贬取决于评价的是务实协调还是牺牲原则。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C007_07 USE:U007_07 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"愿意协调妥协的人","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/cache/epub/11557/pg11557-images.html"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S007_07 USE:U007_07 -->
Morley distinguishes two attitudes: one compromiser conceals what he accepts as truth; the other holds to it without expecting the whole world to follow immediately.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C007_07 -->
莫利区分了两种态度：一种妥协者隐藏自己认可的真理；另一种则坚守它，却不期待全世界立刻跟从。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C007_07 -->
<!-- USE:U007_08 -->
## compromising /ˈkɑmprəmaɪzɪŋ/ adj. · 暴露尴尬秘密而有损名誉的
`常置名词前，信息、照片或处境暴露某人令人难堪或涉及过错的秘密，因而可能损害名誉；不是“善于妥协”的一般形容词。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C007_08 USE:U007_08 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"暴露尴尬秘密而有损名誉的","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/cache/epub/5387/pg5387-images.html"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S007_08 -->
Miss Trevor said she had a photograph of him with something written over it.
<!-- SENTENCE:S007_08_2 USE:U007_08 -->
"Something compromising?" Crocker asked.
<!-- SENTENCE:S007_08_3 -->
"Yes, you would probably call it so," she answered, reddening.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C007_08 -->
特雷弗小姐说，她有一张他的照片，上面还写着一些话。“有损名誉的话？”克罗克问。“是的，你大概会这么说，”她红着脸回答。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C007_08 -->
<!-- USE:U007_09 -->
## compromisingly /ˈkɑmprəmaɪzɪŋli/ adv. · 以可能损害名誉的方式
`描述行为或位置可能使人显得有不当关系、暴露尴尬秘密等；依 compromising 的名誉危险理解，不能自动译成双方妥协地。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C007_09 USE:U007_09 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"以可能损害名誉的方式","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/cache/epub/27439/pg27439-images.html"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S007_09 USE:U007_09 -->
In the conventional blackmail trap described by the narrator, a young woman is the bait, and the intended victim becomes compromisingly entangled with her.
<!-- SENTENCE:S007_09_2 -->
Chappy Marr instead tries to invent a trap that needs no female accomplice.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C007_09 -->
在叙述者描写的惯常勒索圈套中，一名年轻女子被用作诱饵，目标受害者与她陷入足以有损名誉的纠葛。查皮·马尔则试图设计一种不需要女性同谋的圈套。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C007_09 -->
<!-- USE:U007_10 -->
## immunocompromised /ˌɪmjənoʊˈkɑmprəmaɪzd/ adj. · 免疫系统功能受损的
`医学用语，免疫系统功能受到损害；词义本身不指定病因、程度或任何个人治疗方案，也不等于已经感染某种疾病。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C007_10 USE:U007_10 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"免疫系统功能受损的","sourceUrls":["https://www.ons.gov.uk/surveys/informationforhouseholdsandindividuals/householdandindividualsurveys/covid19treatmentsandbehavioursstudyctb"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S007_10 -->
In its information for people invited to the COVID-19 Treatments and Behaviours Study, the ONS explained why their responses mattered.
<!-- SENTENCE:S007_10_2 USE:U007_10 -->
The results would help the UK Health Security Agency understand immunocompromised people’s views on pandemic guidance and their experiences of coronavirus treatments.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C007_10 -->
在发给新冠治疗与行为研究受邀者的说明中，英国国家统计局解释了为什么他们的回答很重要。研究结果将帮助英国卫生安全局了解免疫功能受损者对疫情相关指导的看法，以及他们与新冠治疗相关的经历。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C007_10 -->
<!-- USE:U007_11 -->
## uncompromising /ˌʌnˈkɑmprəmaɪzɪŋ/ adj. · 坚定而不肯让步的
`形容人、立场或行为不愿改变意见或目标，可以赞许坚定，也可批评固执，须看评价语境；不是自动褒义。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C007_11 USE:U007_11 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"坚定而不肯让步的","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/cache/epub/5387/pg5387-images.html"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S007_11 USE:U007_11 -->
"What an uncompromising man you are, Mr. Crocker," she said.
<!-- SENTENCE:S007_11_2 -->
"Once a person is unfortunate enough to come under the ban of your disapproval you have nothing whatever to do with them."
<!-- TRANSLATION:C007_11 -->
“你可真是个毫不让步的人，克罗克先生，”她说。“一个人只要不幸招致你的不满，你就完全不再理睬他了。”
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C007_11 -->
<!-- USE:U007_12 -->
## uncompromising /ˌʌnˈkɑmprəmaɪzɪŋ/ adj. · 不柔化冲击或风格的
`用于艺术、表现或设计等，直接保留令人震惊、不悦或鲜明的部分，不为容易接受而缓和；不只形容谈判立场。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C007_12 USE:U007_12 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"不柔化冲击或风格的","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/cache/epub/73621/pg73621-images.html"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S007_12 -->
Esenwein argues that Maupassant observed life as a realist but selected his material as an artist.
<!-- SENTENCE:S007_12_2 USE:U007_12 -->
He describes Maupassant as uncompromising in his literary presentation and also calls his realism cold and unsympathetic.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C007_12 -->
埃森温认为，莫泊桑像现实主义者那样观察生活，却像艺术家一样选择素材。他形容莫泊桑在文学表现上毫不缓和，同时又称其现实主义风格冷峻而缺少同情。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C007_12 -->
<!-- USE:U007_13 -->
## uncompromisingly /ˌʌnˈkɑmprəmaɪzɪŋli/ adv. · 坚定不让步地
`表示以不改变立场、原则或目标的方式说话或行动，可修饰动词或形容词；仍由语境判断坚定还是顽固。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C007_13 USE:U007_13 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"坚定不让步地","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/cache/epub/67403/pg67403-images.html"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S007_13 -->
In Speer’s account, a schoolboy refuses to join the other boys because his teacher Thring would disapprove.
<!-- SENTENCE:S007_13_2 USE:U007_13 -->
The boy believes he lives before his master’s standards, and by those standards he insists that he will uncompromisingly live.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C007_13 -->
在斯皮尔的叙述中，一名男孩拒绝加入其他男孩的活动，因为他的老师思林不会赞同。男孩相信自己是在老师的标准面前生活，并坚持要毫不妥协地按这些标准行事。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C007_13 -->
<!-- USE:U007_14 -->
## uncompromisingly /ˌʌnˈkɑmprəmaɪzɪŋli/ adv. · 毫不缓和地呈现
`修饰艺术、风格等，表示不软化其强烈、鲜明或难以接受的性质，例如 uncompromisingly modern；不能一概译成正在谈判。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C007_14 USE:U007_14 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"毫不缓和地呈现","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/cache/epub/42383/pg42383-images.html"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S007_14 -->
A 1919 publisher’s notice for The Gods and Mr. Perrin describes a teacher worn down by school drudgery until he publicly rebels against the headmaster.
<!-- SENTENCE:S007_14_2 USE:U007_14 -->
It praises Walpole for painting the school uncompromisingly and unflinchingly, while insisting that the novel’s spirit of revolt keeps it from being merely dreary.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C007_14 -->
1919年的一则出版者荐书文字介绍《诸神与佩林先生》：一位教师被学校的繁重琐务消磨，最终在公开场合反抗校长。荐书文字称赞沃波尔毫不缓和、毫不退缩地描绘这所学校，同时强调小说的反抗精神使它并非一味阴郁乏味。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C007_14 -->
<!-- USE:U007_15 -->
## uncompromisingness /ˌʌnˈkɑmprəmaɪzɪŋnəs/ n. · 坚决不妥协的性质
`不可数，指立场坚决、不让步的性质，也可描述作品不缓和风格的坚持；褒贬随实际对象和语境变化。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C007_15 USE:U007_15 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"坚决不妥协的性质","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/cache/epub/67403/pg67403-images.html"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S007_15_setup -->
Defending the open profession of Christian convictions, Speer refers to Athanasius as a figure regarded as impracticable and troublesome.
<!-- SENTENCE:S007_15 USE:U007_15 -->
Yet, he argues, the world’s progress is often lifted forward by such uncompromisingness.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C007_15 -->
为公开表明基督教信念辩护时，斯皮尔提到，阿塔那修被视为不切实际、令人头疼的人物。但他认为，世界的进步往往正是由这种毫不妥协的精神向前推进的。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C007_15 -->

<!-- WORD:W008 -->
# constrain

**词源**：constrain 源于经法语传入的拉丁语 constringere“紧紧捆住”。这个来源帮助记忆强制、限制；constraint 是相应的约束或受限状态，un- 派生表达免于强制或拘谨。

<!-- USE:U008_01 -->
## constrain /kənˈstreɪn/ v. · 限制行动或发展
`较正式，条件、规则等限制人或事物的行动、发展，直接接宾语，常用 be constrained by…。不等于一定把对方拘押起来。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C008_01 USE:U008_01 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"限制行动或发展","sourceUrls":["https://www.gov.uk/government/consultations/future-of-the-ifrs-9-statutory-override-mitigating-the-impact-of-fair-value-movements-of-pooled-investment-funds/consultation-on-changes-to-the-local-authorities-capital-finance-and-accounting-england-regulations-2003-ifrs-9"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S008_01 -->
In its consultation on local-authority accounting in England, the government addressed a concern that ending an accounting override might encourage riskier investments in property.
<!-- SENTENCE:S008_01_2 USE:U008_01 -->
It considered that risk low, pointing to changes since 2018 designed to constrain investments made primarily for yield.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C008_01 -->
在有关英格兰地方政府会计处理的咨询文件中，政府回应了一项担忧：结束一项会计例外安排，可能会鼓励风险更高的房地产投资。政府认为这种风险较低，并指出，2018年以来的相关调整旨在限制以获取收益为主要目的的投资。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C008_01 -->
<!-- USE:U008_02 -->
## constrain /kənˈstreɪn/ v. · 迫使做不情愿的事
`迫使、促使人采取某行动，可说 constrain somebody to do something；feel/be constrained to do 常指觉得不得不做，尽管本来不愿意，不是“被禁止去做”。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C008_02 USE:U008_02 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"迫使做不情愿的事","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/files/41456/41456-h/41456-h.htm"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S008_02 -->
Frank writes Vera a letter explaining the arrangements for her income and the home she may keep after his departure.
<!-- SENTENCE:S008_02_2 USE:U008_02 -->
Overcome by grief, he is tempted to tear it up, but he regards it as a duty and feels constrained to finish and sign it.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C008_02 -->
弗兰克给薇拉写信，说明自己离开后为她安排的收入，以及她可以继续居住的家。悲痛几次使他想把信撕掉，但他把写完信看作一种责任，觉得不得不写到最后并签名。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C008_02 -->
<!-- USE:U008_03 -->
## constrain /kənˈstreɪn/ v. · 强行束缚或禁闭
`以强制手段约束人身、把人关押或禁闭；区别于预算等抽象限制，不把所有 constrain 都理解为监禁。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C008_03 USE:U008_03 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"强行束缚或禁闭","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/cache/epub/64337/pg64337-images.html"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S008_03 USE:U008_03 -->
In Dryden’s translation of Virgil’s Georgics, Aristaeus rushes at the sleeping Proteus, binds him in chains and constrains his limbs.
<!-- SENTENCE:S008_03_2 -->
Proteus tries fire, beasts and running water to escape, but finally resumes his own shape and speaks.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C008_03 -->
在德莱顿翻译的维吉尔《农事诗》中，阿里斯泰俄斯冲向熟睡的普罗透斯，用锁链捆住他，束缚他的四肢。普罗透斯试着化为火焰、野兽和流水逃走，最终却恢复自己的形貌，开口说话。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C008_03 -->
<!-- USE:U008_04 -->
## constrain /kənˈstreɪn/ v. · 强迫造成某种结果
`及物，强力促成某种表现或结果，重点在结果被迫形成。所选司法语境用 constrain a conclusion 表示某些理由迫使人得出结论；这是该判决中的正式用法，与限制一个动作不让发生有别。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C008_04 USE:U008_04 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"强迫造成某种结果","sourceUrls":["https://www.caseon.in/uploads/cases/pdf/state-of-hp-vs-prem-chand-others.pdf"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S008_04 USE:U008_04 -->
In a 2014 appeal in Himachal Pradesh, the prosecution argued that a daughter’s report of ill-treatment to her father shortly before her death should constrain a conclusion of guilt.
<!-- SENTENCE:S008_04_2 -->
The court, however, questioned the father’s account and upheld the acquittal.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C008_04 -->
在2014年喜马偕尔邦的一宗上诉案中，控方主张，一名女子去世前不久向父亲讲述受虐的情况，理应迫使人得出被告有罪的结论。然而，法院质疑父亲的陈述，并维持了无罪裁判。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C008_04 -->
<!-- USE:U008_05 -->
## constrained /kənˈstreɪnd/ adj. · 受条件限制的
`过去分词作形容词，表示行动、发展或选择受到约束，常见 constrained by…；不是行为一定尴尬。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C008_05 USE:U008_05 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"受条件限制的","sourceUrls":["https://www.gov.uk/government/publications/data-centre-covenant/the-data-centre-covenant"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S008_05 -->
The UK government’s Data Centre Covenant argues that critical services should not rely entirely on infrastructure located overseas.
<!-- SENTENCE:S008_05_2 USE:U008_05 -->
It says domestic data-centre capacity would offer greater resilience if global compute supply became constrained.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C008_05 -->
英国政府的《数据中心公约》主张，关键服务不应完全依赖位于海外的基础设施。文件认为，如果全球算力供应受到限制，国内的数据中心容量将使英国具有更强的应对能力。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C008_05 -->
<!-- USE:U008_06 -->
## constrained /kənˈstreɪnd/ adj. · 勉强不自然的
`用于嗓音、举止等，表示因尴尬、压抑而显得勉强、不自然或拘谨；a constrained voice 不应一概理解成歌唱技巧的假声。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C008_06 USE:U008_06 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"勉强不自然的","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/cache/epub/1342/pg1342-images.html"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S008_06 -->
Elizabeth could not go on.
<!-- SENTENCE:S008_06_2 USE:U008_06 -->
At length Darcy spoke in a constrained manner.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C008_06 -->
伊丽莎白说不下去了。过了一会儿，达西终于开口，态度显得拘谨。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C008_06 -->
<!-- USE:U008_07 -->
## constrainedly /kənˈstreɪnɪdli/ adv. · 拘谨勉强地
`表示以受压抑、不自然、不自在的方式说话或行动；副词中保留/ɪd/音节，不照搬形容词末尾/nd/直接加li。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C008_07 USE:U008_07 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"拘谨勉强地","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/cache/epub/25984/pg25984-images.html"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S008_07_2 -->
Philip asks Sir Randle whether he believes that Philip and Titterton could be guilty of such knavery.
<!-- SENTENCE:S008_07 USE:U008_07 -->
"Frankly—I must be frank—I hardly know what to believe," Sir Randle replies constrainedly.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C008_07 -->
菲利普问兰德尔爵士，是否相信他和蒂特顿会干出这种欺诈勾当。兰德尔爵士拘谨地回答：“坦白说——我得坦白说——我简直不知道该相信什么。”
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C008_07 -->
<!-- USE:U008_08 -->
## constraint /kənˈstreɪnt/ n. · 限制行动的条件
`可数，限制、控制行动或运动的条件、因素，常用 financial/time constraints、constraints on…；不是作动词“强迫”。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C008_08 USE:U008_08 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"限制行动的条件","sourceUrls":["https://www.gov.uk/government/publications/research-on-the-cyber-security-of-ai/cyber-security-risks-to-artificial-intelligence"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S008_08 -->
A 2024 report on cyber security risks to AI used interviews to ground its assessment in organisations’ practical experience.
<!-- SENTENCE:S008_08_2 USE:U008_08 -->
The report says only seven of the seventeen organisations invited were interviewed, with time constraints or organisational policies leading to withdrawals.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C008_08 -->
一份2024年有关人工智能网络安全风险的报告通过访谈，使评估立足于机构的实际经验。报告称，受邀的十七家机构中只有七家接受了访谈，时间限制或机构内部政策导致了一些退出。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C008_08 -->
<!-- USE:U008_09 -->
## constraint /kənˈstreɪnt/ n. · 强制约束与禁闭
`指约束、受约束或自由被限制的行为或状态，通常不可数，可包括人身禁闭；并非每次都指一条具体规则。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C008_09 USE:U008_09 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"强制约束与禁闭","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/cache/epub/55614/pg55614-images.html"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S008_09 -->
In his 1856 account of the Bayeux Tapestry, J. Collingwood Bruce describes William demanding the crown and reminding Harold of his oath.
<!-- SENTENCE:S008_09_2 USE:U008_09 -->
Bruce presents Harold’s reply as an admission that he took the oath under constraint, with a promise he could not fulfil against his country’s will.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C008_09 -->
在1856年解说贝叶挂毯的著作中，J.柯林伍德·布鲁斯描述威廉要求交出王位，并提醒哈罗德曾经立誓。布鲁斯笔下哈罗德的答复承认自己曾在强迫之下起誓，同时说明那项承诺违背本国意愿，无法履行。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C008_09 -->
<!-- USE:U008_10 -->
## constraint /kənˈstreɪnt/ n. · 克制情感或拘谨的状态
`自然感情及其表达受抑制，或举止不自在的状态；区别于可数的资金、时间限制。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C008_10 USE:U008_10 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"克制情感或拘谨的状态","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/cache/epub/98/pg98-images.html"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S008_10 USE:U008_10 -->
Doctor Manette’s constraint was so obvious, and so plainly came from his unwillingness to approach the subject, that Charles Darnay hesitated.
<!-- SENTENCE:S008_10_2 -->
"Shall I go on, sir?" Darnay asked.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C008_10 -->
马奈特医生的拘谨如此明显，而且显然源于他不愿触及这个话题，以至于查尔斯·达尔内犹豫了。“先生，我还继续说吗？”达尔内问。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C008_10 -->
<!-- USE:U008_11 -->
## unconstrained /ˌʌnkənˈstreɪnd/ adj. · 不受限制或强制的
`不受外在约束、强迫，能自由行动或选择；用于行为可表示自愿，不保证行为本身合理或没有后果。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C008_11 USE:U008_11 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"不受限制或强制的","sourceUrls":["https://www.gov.uk/government/publications/property-guardians-headline-report/property-guardians-headline-report"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S008_11 -->
A study of property guardianship in England interviewed twenty-four people who lived in buildings partly to help keep them secure.
<!-- SENTENCE:S008_11_2 USE:U008_11 -->
Guardianship was rarely described as an unconstrained choice, yet some participants welcomed the unusual buildings and freedom to personalise their living space.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C008_11 -->
一项关于英格兰房产看护居住安排的研究访谈了二十四位居住者；他们入住这些建筑，也承担着帮助保障房产安全的作用。受访者很少把这种安排说成不受限制的自由选择，但有些人喜欢这些不寻常的建筑，也喜欢能够按自己的想法布置居住空间。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C008_11 -->
<!-- USE:U008_12 -->
## unconstrained /ˌʌnkənˈstreɪnd/ adj. · 从容自然不拘谨的
`形容举止或表达轻松、自然、无勉强，不是法律或数学中没有约束条件。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C008_12 USE:U008_12 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"从容自然不拘谨的","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/cache/epub/53551/pg53551-images.html"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S008_12 -->
"I have made up my mind in regard to you, Ferdinand."
<!-- SENTENCE:S008_12_2 USE:U008_12 -->
She used his first name with the unconstrained freedom of their relationship.
<!-- SENTENCE:S008_12_3 -->
"I will not have your help. You shall not be involved through me."
<!-- TRANSLATION:C008_12 -->
“关于你的事，我已经决定了，费迪南德。”她直呼他的名字，带着他们这种关系所允许的自然无拘。“我不要你的帮助。你不能因为我而卷进去。”
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C008_12 -->
<!-- USE:U008_13 -->
## unconstrainedly /ˌʌnkənˈstreɪnɪdli/ adv. · 自由而不受强制地
`以不受限制、不被强迫的方式行动，可随语境表示自愿；保留/ɪd/音节。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C008_13 USE:U008_13 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"自由而不受强制地","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/cache/epub/42932/pg42932-images.html"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S008_13 -->
In Guthrie’s translation, Plotinus begins an inquiry into free will by asking what it means for an action to depend on us.
<!-- SENTENCE:S008_13_2 USE:U008_13 -->
He calls an act voluntary when we do it unconstrainedly and consciously, then distinguishes this from merely having the power to act or refrain.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C008_13 -->
在格思里的译本中，普罗提诺探讨自由意志时，首先问：一项行动取决于我们，究竟意味着什么？他把我们不受强迫而且有意识地做出的行为称为自愿行为，随后将其与仅仅有能力去做或不做某事区分开来。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C008_13 -->
<!-- USE:U008_14 -->
## unconstrainedly /ˌʌnkənˈstreɪnɪdli/ adv. · 从容自然地
`以没有拘谨、勉强或压抑的方式表现。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C008_14 USE:U008_14 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"从容自然地","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/cache/epub/53551/pg53551-images.html"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S008_14 -->
"You speak excellent Spanish, senor. May I ask your name?"
<!-- SENTENCE:S008_14_2 USE:U008_14 -->
"Is that a crime?" the narrator asked with a sneer, while Mayhew laughed unconstrainedly and most naturally.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C008_14 -->
“先生，你西班牙语说得很好。我可以问问你的名字吗？”“那是犯罪吗？”叙述者冷笑着反问，梅休则毫无拘谨、十分自然地笑了起来。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C008_14 -->
<!-- USE:U008_15 -->
## unconstraint /ˌʌnkənˈstreɪnt/ n. · 自由自在无拘束
`不可数，免于限制、强迫或拘谨的状态，依语境可指自愿和从容自在；不是具体一条“反向约束”。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C008_15 USE:U008_15 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"自由自在无拘束","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/files/119/old/old/orig119-h/p7.htm"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S008_15 -->
In his comic essay on German, Twain imagines a foreign learner filling gaps with convenient words and buying time by saying ALSO.
<!-- SENTENCE:S008_15_2 USE:U008_15 -->
He jokes that scattering ALSO’s or you knows through conversation gives it an air of grace, elegance and unconstraint.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C008_15 -->
在戏谈德语的文章中，吐温设想外国学习者用几个方便的词填补表达空缺，再说一句ALSO给自己争取思考时间。他打趣说，把ALSO或者you know撒进谈话里，便能让谈吐显得优雅而从容自在。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C008_15 -->

<!-- WORD:W009 -->
# convey

**词源**：convey 经古法语 conveier 一路发展，关联“同行、护送”与拉丁语 via“道路”。从运送发展出信息传达和法律转让；conveyance 既可指过程，也可指交通工具或转让文书，不能只按后缀猜一个义。

<!-- USE:U009_01 -->
## convey /kənˈveɪ/ v. · 传达信息或感受
`使想法、消息、含义或感受为人知道、理解，直接接宾语，可用 convey something to somebody；并非只能通过语言表达，图画、动作等也可传情达意。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C009_01 USE:U009_01 KIND:excerpt -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"传达信息或感受","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/cache/epub/1342/pg1342-images.html"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S009_01 USE:U009_01 -->
"My ideas flow so rapidly that I have not time to express them; by which means my letters sometimes convey no ideas at all to my correspondents."
<!-- TRANSLATION:C009_01 -->
“我的想法涌得太快，来不及表达，所以我的信有时根本没能向收信人传达什么想法。”
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C009_01 -->
<!-- USE:U009_02 -->
## convey /kənˈveɪ/ v. · 运送人或物
`较正式，载送、运输人或货物到某处，常用 convey somebody/something to…；与表达消息的比喻义区别。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C009_02 USE:U009_02 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"运送人或物","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/cache/epub/16593/pg16593-images.html"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S009_02 USE:U009_02 -->
In Clark’s historical example of Denver’s gravity water-supply system, pipes convey water from mountain streams to the city.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C009_02 -->
在克拉克关于丹佛重力供水系统的历史示例中，管道把山间溪水输送到城里。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C009_02 -->
<!-- USE:U009_03 -->
## convey /kənˈveɪ/ v. · 转让财产权属
`法律用语，把财产的所有权转给另一方，直接接 property/land 等；不是仅把物品运走，也不等于租赁。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C009_03 USE:U009_03 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"转让财产权属","sourceUrls":["https://www.gov.uk/government/publications/compulsory-purchase-process-guidance/guidance-on-the-compulsory-purchase-process"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S009_03 USE:U009_03 -->
Guidance for acquiring authorities in England asks how charity trustees can convey land to a public authority.
<!-- SENTENCE:S009_03_2 -->
It advises authorities acquiring land from a charity to be aware of Part 7 of the Charities Act 2011 and says they may need to consult the Charity Commission.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C009_03 -->
一份给英格兰土地征收机构的指南提出：慈善机构的受托人如何把土地所有权转让给公共机构？指南提醒从慈善机构取得土地的征收机构注意《2011年慈善法》第七部分的规定，并指出，他们可能需要咨询慈善委员会。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C009_03 -->
<!-- USE:U009_04 -->
## convey /kənˈveɪ/ v. · 传导声音气味等
`表示声音、气味等经媒介传送、传播；与有意向人表达观点分开。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C009_04 USE:U009_04 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"传导声音气味等","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/files/54897/54897-h/54897-h.htm"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S009_04 USE:U009_04 -->
When sound is conveyed through the air, Herschel explains, each moving particle urges its neighbour forward and then retreats.
<!-- SENTENCE:S009_04_2 -->
This transfers motion onwards while the particles themselves move back and forth.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C009_04 -->
赫歇尔解释说，声音经空气传播时，运动的微粒推动邻近微粒向前，随后又退回。运动就这样不断向前传递，而微粒本身来回振动。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C009_04 -->
<!-- USE:U009_05 -->
## conveyable /kənˈveɪəbəl/ adj. · 可传送或转让的
`表示能够被运送、传达、传播，法律语境也可指财产权属可以转让；具体随被修饰对象确定，不暗示已经完成。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C009_05 USE:U009_05 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"可传送或转让的","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/files/15043/15043-h/15043-h.htm"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S009_05 -->
Burke asks why eloquence and poetry can make such deep impressions on the passions.
<!-- SENTENCE:S009_05_2 USE:U009_05 -->
He argues that our feelings about things are shaped by other people’s opinions, conveyable for the most part by words alone.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C009_05 -->
伯克探讨，为什么雄辩和诗歌能给情感留下如此深刻的印象。他认为，我们对事物的感受受到他人看法的影响，而那些看法大多只能通过语言传达。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C009_05 -->
<!-- USE:U009_06 -->
## conveyance /kənˈveɪəns/ n. · 运输运送的过程
`较正式，运送人或物的行为、过程，通常不可数，常用 conveyance of…；不是必然指某一辆车。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C009_06 USE:U009_06 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"运输运送的过程","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/cache/epub/66597/pg66597-images.html"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S009_06 USE:U009_06 -->
Gilbey describes carts used for the conveyance of agricultural produce on early English roads.
<!-- SENTENCE:S009_06_2 -->
He illustrates the difficulty of winter transport with a 1537 report that lead from Jervaulx Abbey could not be moved until summer because the roads were too muddy for a cart.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C009_06 -->
吉尔比描述了在英国早期道路上用于运送农产品的货车。他引用1537年一份关于杰瓦尔克斯修道院的报告来说明冬季运输之难：道路太过泥泞，连货车也无法通过，屋顶铅材要等到夏天才能运走。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C009_06 -->
<!-- USE:U009_07 -->
## conveyance /kənˈveɪəns/ n. · 交通运输工具
`可数，指运送人的车辆或其他交通工具，文学语体中可用这种概括称呼；不把它理解为一份法律文件。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C009_07 USE:U009_07 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"交通运输工具","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/cache/epub/66597/pg66597-images.html"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S009_07 USE:U009_07 -->
If the old illustrations accurately show their proportions and construction, these Anglo-Saxon carriages must have been very uncomfortable conveyances.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C009_07 -->
如果旧插图准确呈现了它们的比例与结构，那么这些盎格鲁-撒克逊马车一定是很不舒适的交通工具。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C009_07 -->
<!-- USE:U009_08 -->
## conveyance /kənˈveɪəns/ n. · 观点意思的传达
`观点、思想或含义被表达、传递的行为，通常不可数；不是搬运承载这些信息的纸张。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C009_08 USE:U009_08 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"观点意思的传达","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/cache/epub/10616/pg10616-images.html"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S009_08 -->
Even if a sacred text is held to be infallibly true, Locke argues, its reader can still misunderstand it.
<!-- SENTENCE:S009_08_2 USE:U009_08 -->
When meaning is clothed in words, that sort of conveyance remains subject to doubt and uncertainty.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C009_08 -->
洛克认为，即便一篇神圣文本被认为绝对真实，读者也仍可能误解它。意义一旦借助文字表达，这种传达方式就仍会受到疑惑和不确定性的影响。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C009_08 -->
<!-- USE:U009_09 -->
## conveyance /kənˈveɪəns/ n. · 声音等的传导
`声音等通过媒介传播的过程；区别于面向听者传达思想的交际行为。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C009_09 USE:U009_09 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"声音等的传导","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/files/52869/old/52869-h/52869-h.htm"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S009_09 USE:U009_09 -->
Somerville calls the conveyance of sound one of the atmosphere’s important functions.
<!-- SENTENCE:S009_09_2 -->
She compares sound waves with waves passing across a field of corn, then marks a difference: the wind moves each stalk, while vibrating air particles transmit their motion to their neighbours.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C009_09 -->
萨默维尔把声音的传导称为大气的一项重要作用。她把声波比作掠过麦田的波动，同时指出一处区别：麦秆各自受风推动，而振动的空气微粒会把运动传给邻近微粒。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C009_09 -->
<!-- USE:U009_10 -->
## conveyance /kənˈveɪəns/ n. · 财产所有权转让
`法律语境，把财产由一位所有人转给另一位的行为或手续，不等于实际搬家或一般车辆运输。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C009_10 USE:U009_10 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"财产所有权转让","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/files/28253/28253-h/28253-h.htm"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S009_10 -->
MacMillan records that papers for transferring the McGill estate, excluding the endowment fund, to the Royal Institution were completed in May 1820.
<!-- SENTENCE:S009_10_2 USE:U009_10 -->
The conveyance followed on 7 June, and the deed was recorded on 3 August, yet possession remained disputed.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C009_10 -->
麦克米伦记载，把麦吉尔地产移交给皇家教育促进机构（Royal Institution）的文件于1820年5月办妥，但此次移交不包括捐赠基金。产权转让随后于6月7日完成，契据于8月3日登记，不过实际占有地产的问题仍有争议。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C009_10 -->
<!-- USE:U009_11 -->
## conveyance /kənˈveɪəns/ n. · 财产权转让文书
`法律语境，可数，产生财产权转让效力的文书；与转让行为本身分开，an old conveyance 可指旧契据。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C009_11 USE:U009_11 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"财产权转让文书","sourceUrls":["https://www.gov.uk/government/publications/first-registrations/practice-guide-1-first-registrations"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S009_11 USE:U009_11 -->
For a first registration in England and Wales, if the title deeds contain a satisfactory plan, mark the second box and state the nature and date of the deed containing it—for example, "conveyance dated 21 April 1926".
<!-- SENTENCE:S009_11_2 -->
Use this option only where the deed plan is well drawn to an adequate scale and it is quite clear that the position of the boundaries has not changed since the plan was prepared.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C009_11 -->
在英格兰和威尔士办理首次登记时，如果产权契据内有合格的土地平面图，就勾选第二个方框，并写明含该图的契据性质与日期，例如“1926年4月21日的财产转让文书”。只有在契据中的平面图绘制良好、比例尺适当，而且能够十分清楚地确认边界位置自制图以来未曾改变时，才应选择这种方式。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C009_11 -->
<!-- USE:U009_12 -->
## conveyancer /kənˈveɪənsɚ/ n. · 办理财产转让的专业人员
`可数，办理不动产等财产所有权转移手续的人；资格、可办理事项依地区制度，不必都称出庭律师，也不是货运代理。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C009_12 USE:U009_12 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"办理财产转让的专业人员","sourceUrls":["https://www.gov.uk/selling-a-home/offers-negotiations"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S009_12 USE:U009_12 -->
In England and Wales, once you have accepted an offer, the buyer’s solicitor or conveyancer will arrange searches and a review of the property’s title.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C009_12 -->
在英格兰和威尔士，一旦你接受了报价，买方的律师或产权转让专业人员就会安排查询，并审查房产的产权。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C009_12 -->
<!-- USE:U009_13 -->
## conveyancing /kənˈveɪənsɪŋ/ n. · 财产权转让法律业务
`通常不可数，处理财产所有权转移手续的业务或过程，主要英式法律用语；不是货物配送。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C009_13 USE:U009_13 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"财产权转让法律业务","sourceUrls":["https://www.gov.uk/buying-a-home/transferring-ownership"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S009_13 USE:U009_13 -->
In the GOV.UK guide to conveyancing, once an offer is accepted, the seller is responsible for drawing up the legal contract to transfer ownership.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C009_13 -->
在 GOV.UK 的产权转让业务指南中，报价获接受后，卖方负责拟定转移所有权的法律合同。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C009_13 -->
<!-- USE:U009_14 -->
## conveyor /kənˈveɪɚ/ n.; conveyer /kənˈveɪɚ/ n. · 传递运送的人或物
`两种拼写均可，表示进行 convey 动作的人或物；可以是信息的传递者，不一定是一条机器传送带。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C009_14 USE:U009_14 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"传递运送的人或物","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/files/70188/70188-h/70188-h.htm"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S009_14 USE:U009_14 -->
Writing in 1916, Ernest A. Dench proposes a nickname for the motion picture: a conveyor of things as they are.
<!-- SENTENCE:S009_14_2 -->
He urges food manufacturers to use films to take audiences through their plants when visits in person are inconvenient.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C009_14 -->
1916年，欧内斯特·A.登奇为电影提议了一个别称：如实呈现事物的传递媒介。他敦促食品制造商用电影带观众参观工厂，因为亲自参观往往并不方便。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C009_14 -->
<!-- USE:U009_15 -->
## conveyor /kənˈveɪɚ/ n.; conveyer /kənˈveɪɚ/ n. · 连续运料的传送设备
`可数，运送材料的装置；工厂语境中 conveyor 常可简称 conveyor belt，也可指其他传送设备，不能反向认定所有conveyor都只有皮带。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C009_15 USE:U009_15 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"连续运料的传送设备","sourceUrls":["https://www.osha.gov/laws-regs/regulations/standardnumber/1926/1926.555"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S009_15 USE:U009_15 -->
Under OSHA’s construction regulation, an emergency-stop switch must prevent a conveyor from restarting until that switch has been reset to the running or "on" position.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C009_15 -->
按照 OSHA 的建筑施工规定，急停开关必须能阻止传送设备重新启动，直到该开关被复位到运行或“开”的位置。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C009_15 -->
<!-- USE:U009_16 -->
## conveyor belt /kənˈveɪɚ bɛlt/ n. · 循环运转的传送带
`可数，连续循环移动的橡胶或金属等带子，用于工厂等处运送物品或材料；重点是带状连续运送。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C009_16 USE:U009_16 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"循环运转的传送带","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/files/46094/old/46094-h/46094-h.htm"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S009_16 USE:U009_16 -->
Williams describes grain handling at Liverpool’s Alexander Grain Warehouse: after weighing, grain lands on an endless, flat conveyer belt.
<!-- SENTENCE:S009_16_2 -->
The belt takes it along the quay and drops it onto a second belt running at right angles, which carries it towards the storehouse.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C009_16 -->
威廉斯描述利物浦亚历山大粮食仓库的谷物搬运流程：称重之后，谷物落到一条循环运转的平直传送带上。传送带沿码头把谷物送出，再将其落到与第一条成直角的第二条带上，由后者把谷物送向仓库。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C009_16 -->
<!-- USE:U009_17 -->
## conveyor belt /kənˈveɪɚ bɛlt/ n. · 把人事机械同样处理的流水线
`比喻一种总把人或事按相同方式处理、产出同样结果的制度或过程，通常带不赞成；不是只要高效率就有这种负面含义。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C009_17 USE:U009_17 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"把人事机械同样处理的流水线","sourceUrls":["https://www.gov.uk/government/publications/the-terrorism-acts-in-2020/the-terrorism-acts-in-2020-report-of-the-independent-reviewer-of-terrorism-legislation-accessible-version"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S009_17 USE:U009_17 -->
Reviewing the operation of terrorism laws in 2020, Jonathan Hall warns examining officers against conveyor-belt decision-making when considering digital downloads.
<!-- SENTENCE:S009_17_2 -->
He says budgetary pressure can create incentives to use powers simply to demonstrate productivity.
<!-- SENTENCE:S009_17_3 -->
Officers should not be encouraged to exercise them on a use-it-or-lose-it basis.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C009_17 -->
乔纳森·霍尔在审查2020年反恐法律的实施时，告诫检查人员：考虑提取数字资料时，要避免流水线式的决策。他指出，预算压力可能诱使人员仅为显示工作产出而行使权力。不应鼓励他们抱着“不用就会失去”的心态来动用这些权力。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C009_17 -->

<!-- WORD:W010 -->
# credible

**词源**：credible 源于拉丁语 credibilis，来自 credere“相信”，-ible 表可以。credibility 是可信的性质；incredible 的否定来源发展出“不可信”与口语“惊人、极好”两路，不能看到in-就把所有赞叹译成否定。

<!-- USE:U010_01 -->
## credible /ˈkrɛdəbəl/ adj. · 值得相信的
`人、说法或证据等可靠、值得相信，可用 credible to somebody；不等于事实已经证明，也不是“轻信的”credulous。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C010_01 USE:U010_01 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"值得相信的","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/cache/epub/6473/pg6473-images.html"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S010_01 -->
Your sources must satisfy the audience, not merely seem good to you.
<!-- SENTENCE:S010_01_2 USE:U010_01 -->
The audience judges what is credible, so explain why an important or disputed witness is reliable.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C010_01 -->
你的来源必须能让听众信服，而不能只是你自己觉得好。听众会判断什么值得相信，因此遇到重要或可能受质疑的证人时，要解释其为何可靠。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C010_01 -->
<!-- USE:U010_02 -->
## credible /ˈkrɛdəbəl/ adj. · 威胁等令人信其会实现的
`威胁、承诺等使人相信说话者有能力或意图付诸实施；credible threat 不等于威胁已经执行。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C010_02 USE:U010_02 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"威胁等令人信其会实现的","sourceUrls":["https://www.gov.uk/guidance/the-2021-integrated-review-nuclear-frequently-asked-questions"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S010_02 USE:U010_02 -->
The UK’s 2021 Integrated Review confirmed a commitment to a minimum credible independent nuclear deterrent.
<!-- SENTENCE:S010_02_2 -->
The government’s FAQ presented the decision as a response to the international security environment and the actions of potential adversaries.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C010_02 -->
英国2021年综合评估确认了承诺：维持最低限度、可信的独立核威慑。政府的问答把这项决定解释为对国际安全环境和潜在对手行动的回应。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C010_02 -->
<!-- USE:U010_03 -->
## credible /ˈkrɛdəbəl/ adj. · 有成功可能而值得认真对待的
`用于候选人、政策、制度等，表示看起来有能力奏效或成功，足以成为认真的选择；不保证最终胜出。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C010_03 USE:U010_03 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"有成功可能而值得认真对待的","sourceUrls":["https://www.gov.uk/guidance/the-2021-integrated-review-nuclear-frequently-asked-questions"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S010_03 USE:U010_03 -->
In its 2021-review FAQ, the UK government states that it remains committed to the Non-Proliferation Treaty and says, "There is no credible alternative route to nuclear disarmament."
<!-- TRANSLATION:C010_03 -->
在关于2021年综合评估的问答中，英国政府表示自己仍致力于《不扩散核武器条约》，并称：“没有其他可信可行的核裁军途径。”
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C010_03 -->
<!-- USE:U010_04 -->
## credibility /ˌkrɛdəˈbɪləti/ n. · 可信性与信任度
`通常不可数，人或事物值得相信以及受信任的程度；可说 gain/lose/maintain credibility，不等于已经核实每一项说法。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C010_04 USE:U010_04 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"可信性与信任度","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/cache/epub/6473/pg6473-images.html"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S010_04 USE:U010_04 -->
Test both the witness and the statement: consider the witness’s truthfulness, intelligence and experience, and the statement’s consistency and general credibility.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C010_04 -->
要同时检验证人和陈述：考察证人的诚实、理解能力和经验，以及陈述的一致性与总体可信度。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C010_04 -->
<!-- USE:U010_05 -->
## credibility /ˌkrɛdəˈbɪləti/ n. · 名声地位与认可度
`指声誉、身份或被认可的地位，重点不只在一句话是真是假；在特定青年文化语境中可指 street credibility。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C010_05 USE:U010_05 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"名声地位与认可度","sourceUrls":["https://assets.publishing.service.gov.uk/media/5a799388ed915d04220697e7/12-1315-evaluation-capital-expenditure-in-further-education-colleges.pdf"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S010_05 USE:U010_05 -->
In a 2012 study of capital spending in English further-education colleges, one suburban college reported increased credibility with employers because its new building offered industry-standard facilities.
<!-- SENTENCE:S010_05_2 -->
The report cautions that colleges generally lacked hard evidence to support their employer-engagement claims.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C010_05 -->
在2012年一项对英格兰继续教育学院基建支出的研究中，一所郊区学院表示，新校舍能提供符合行业标准的设施，因此提高了它在雇主眼中的认可度。报告提醒，学院通常缺乏足够的硬证据来支持它们关于雇主参与改善的说法。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C010_05 -->
<!-- USE:U010_06 -->
## credibility gap /ˌkrɛdəˈbɪləti ɡæp/ n. · 说辞与事实行动的落差
`通常作单数，某人说的、承诺的与实际想的、做的或事实之间看得出的差距，由此造成不信任；不是泛指任何两人意见不同。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C010_06 USE:U010_06 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"说辞与事实行动的落差","sourceUrls":["https://www.gov.uk/government/speeches/scottish-secretary-speech-on-the-scottish-independence-debate"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S010_06 USE:U010_06 -->
In the published script of a 2013 speech opposing Scottish independence, Alistair Carmichael calls for the planned White Paper to bridge a credibility gap.
<!-- SENTENCE:S010_06_2 -->
He argues that promises about shared institutions offer false reassurance because key arrangements would require negotiation and could not be guaranteed in advance.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C010_06 -->
在一份2013年反对苏格兰独立的演说文稿中，阿利斯泰尔·卡迈克尔要求拟议白皮书弥合相关计划的言实落差。他认为，关于继续共享制度安排的承诺给人以虚假安慰，因为关键安排需要谈判，无法事先保证。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C010_06 -->
<!-- USE:U010_07 -->
## credibly /ˈkrɛdəbli/ adv. · 可信而令人信服地
`以值得相信、足以让人信服的方式陈述或行动；不表示听者已经证明其为真。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C010_07 USE:U010_07 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"可信而令人信服地","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/cache/epub/44286/pg44286-images.html"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S010_07 -->
Steven follows reports of a fiddler from village to village in Thuringia.
<!-- SENTENCE:S010_07_2 USE:U010_07 -->
He is credibly informed that the man usually roams the forest at that time of year, yet each fresh lead says he has only just passed.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C010_07 -->
史蒂文循着一名提琴手的消息，在图林根的村庄间辗转寻找。可靠的消息告诉他，那人每年这个时候常在林区游荡，可每一条新线索都说，提琴手刚刚才经过。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C010_07 -->
<!-- USE:U010_08 -->
## incredible /ɪnˈkrɛdəbəl/ adj. · 好得令人赞叹的
`对人或物极好、非同寻常的赞许，常见于非正式评价；此义不表示认定对方说谎。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C010_08 USE:U010_08 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"好得令人赞叹的","sourceUrls":["https://www.gov.uk/government/speeches/michael-gove-at-the-national-college-annual-conference"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S010_08 USE:U010_08 -->
At a teachers’ conference in June 2012, Michael Gove praised the incredible performance of new sponsored academies in parts of London.
<!-- SENTENCE:S010_08_2 -->
He used their results to argue for expanding the programme, while also crediting stronger teaching and support between schools.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C010_08 -->
2012年6月，迈克尔·戈夫在教师会议上赞扬伦敦部分地区新设赞助型学院制学校的出色表现。他用这些成绩支持扩大该项目，同时也肯定了教学改善和学校之间相互支持的作用。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C010_08 -->
<!-- USE:U010_09 -->
## incredible /ɪnˈkrɛdəbəl/ adj. · 异常惊人而难以置信的
`令人非常意外，几乎难以相信，但事情仍可能真实；可用 It is incredible that…，与明确不值得相信的义分开。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C010_09 USE:U010_09 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"异常惊人而难以置信的","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/cache/epub/1342/pg1342-images.html"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S010_09 USE:U010_09 -->
To Mrs. Hurst and Miss Bingley, it was almost incredible that Elizabeth had walked three miles so early, alone and in such dirty weather.
<!-- SENTENCE:S010_09_2 -->
They nevertheless received her very politely.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C010_09 -->
赫斯特太太和宾利小姐几乎无法相信，伊丽莎白这么早竟独自在这样恶劣的天气中走了三英里。不过，她们仍很有礼貌地接待了她。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C010_09 -->
<!-- USE:U010_10 -->
## incredible /ɪnˈkrɛdəbəl/ adj. · 程度数量极大的
`用来强调程度、数量或强度极不寻常，可修饰名词或用于 it is incredible how…；不必含赞许，也可能是极大困难。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C010_10 USE:U010_10 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"程度数量极大的","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/files/23860/23860-h/23860-h.htm"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S010_10 -->
Alcott urges his young readers to give each hour a purpose and keep to the time set for a task.
<!-- SENTENCE:S010_10_2 USE:U010_10 -->
He argues that systematic habits let some men perform an incredible amount of business while still enjoying abundant leisure.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C010_10 -->
奥尔科特劝年轻读者为每个小时安排用途，并按预定时间做事。他认为，有条理的习惯能让一些人处理极大量的事务，同时仍有充裕的闲暇。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C010_10 -->
<!-- USE:U010_11 -->
## incredible /ɪnˈkrɛdəbəl/ adj. · 无法相信其为真的
`不可信、不能相信，针对真实性作否定判断；与口语“惊人但真实”不同，需看论证和上下文。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C010_11 USE:U010_11 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"无法相信其为真的","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/files/30866/30866-h/30866-h.htm"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S010_11 -->
In his analysis of Mill’s logic, Stebbing explains why evidence can sometimes lead to disbelief rather than a mere suspension of judgement.
<!-- SENTENCE:S010_11_2 USE:U010_11 -->
A claim contradicting a rigorously established universal rule is incredible, he argues, unless an adequate counteracting cause or a better-established law changes the case.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C010_11 -->
斯特宾在分析密尔的逻辑学时，解释为什么证据有时会使人不相信，而不只是暂缓判断。他认为，若一项说法违背严格确立的普遍规律，它就不可信，除非存在足以抵消原有作用的原因，或有更充分确立的规律改变这一判断。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C010_11 -->
<!-- USE:U010_12 -->
## incredibility /ɪnˌkrɛdəˈbɪləti/ n. · 不可信或难以置信的性质
`表示某事无法相信或极难相信的性质；可强调证词不可信，也可强调事情出奇，须随上下文区分，不因前缀in-就译成可信。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C010_12 USE:U010_12 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"不可信或难以置信的性质","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/cache/epub/40983/pg40983-images.html"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S010_12 USE:U010_12 -->
The anonymous critic charges the Bible with obscurity, incredibility and contradiction, judging it as a human composition rather than accepting claims of divine authority.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C010_12 -->
这位匿名批评者指责《圣经》晦涩、不可信并存在矛盾，把它视为人的作品来评判，而不接受其神圣权威的主张。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C010_12 -->
<!-- USE:U010_13 -->
## incredibly /ɪnˈkrɛdəbli/ adv. · 极其非常
`强调形容词或副词的程度，可用于好坏两面，如 incredibly good-looking/hard；不总表示怀疑真实性。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C010_13 USE:U010_13 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"极其非常","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/cache/epub/38776/pg38776-images.html"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S010_13 USE:U010_13 -->
Thinking of Fanny among wide verandahs, peaches and cream, and Atlantic breezes, James writes that the picture makes him "quite incredibly homesick".
<!-- TRANSLATION:C010_13 -->
想到范妮身处宽阔游廊、桃子配奶油和大西洋微风之间，詹姆斯写道，这幅图景让他“想家想得厉害极了”。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C010_13 -->
<!-- USE:U010_14 -->
## incredibly /ɪnˈkrɛdəbli/ adv. · 令人难以置信的是
`作为句子评注，表示所述事情令人惊讶，往往置于句首加逗号；不是一律表示不是真的。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C010_14 USE:U010_14 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"令人难以置信的是","sourceUrls":["https://imagine.gsfc.nasa.gov/news/10sep08.html"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S010_14 -->
NASA’s 2008 account of GRB 080319B says the burst brightened enough to be visible to the unaided eye in a dark sky.
<!-- SENTENCE:S010_14_2 USE:U010_14 -->
Incredibly, the dying star was 7.5 billion light-years away.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C010_14 -->
NASA在2008年对GRB 080319B的报道中说，这次爆发一度亮到在黑暗夜空中凭肉眼即可看见。令人难以置信的是，那颗垂死的恒星距我们75亿光年。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C010_14 -->
<!-- USE:U010_15 -->
## incredibly /ɪnˈkrɛdəbli/ adv. · 以不可信的方式
`修饰作证、叙述等时，可表示说得不可信，而非纯粹加强程度；须由语境确认是在评价可信度。证言因记忆不可靠而不被采信，也可用此义，不必然指说话者故意捏造。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C010_15 USE:U010_15 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"以不可信的方式","sourceUrls":["https://cases.justia.com/pennsylvania/superior-court/2014-1521-wda-2013.pdf?ts=1410892913"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S010_15 USE:U010_15 -->
In a 2014 non-precedential decision about a loan dispute, Pennsylvania’s Superior Court upheld a finding that Novak had testified incredibly.
<!-- SENTENCE:S010_15_2 -->
It explained that unreliable recollection could justify discounting testimony, even without a finding that she had fabricated it.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C010_15 -->
在2014年一宗借款纠纷的非先例性裁判中，宾夕法尼亚州高等法院维持了诺瓦克所作证言不可信的认定。法院解释说，记忆不可靠可以成为不采信证言的理由，即使并未认定她捏造了证言。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C010_15 -->
<!-- USE:U010_16 -->
## street cred /ˌstrit ˈkrɛd/ n.; street credibility /ˌstrit ˌkrɛdəˈbɪləti/ n.; street-cred /ˌstrit ˈkrɛd/ n. · 获青年街头文化认同的声望
`通常不可数，英式用语，street cred及带连字符拼写较口语，常带赞许；指因时尚、观点或文化归属被一般城市青年认作自己人。不是道路工程的信用评级，也不只等同学术证据可信度；credibility 在此语境也可单独使用。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C010_16 USE:U010_16 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"获青年街头文化认同的声望","sourceUrls":["https://assets.publishing.service.gov.uk/government/uploads/system/uploads/attachment_data/file/535032/rr922-qualitative-evaluation-of-the-dwp-innovation-fund-final-report.pdf"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S010_16 USE:U010_16 -->
A 2016 qualitative evaluation of the DWP Innovation Fund reports that key workers familiar with local language and youth culture had street credibility and were seen as cool by young people.
<!-- SENTENCE:S010_16_2 -->
School staff believed those qualities helped them re-engage the most disaffected participants.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C010_16 -->
一份2016年对英国就业与养老金部创新基金的定性评估报告说，熟悉本地用语和青年文化的辅导人员具有街头文化认同的声望，被年轻人视为很酷。学校员工认为，这些特质对重新吸引最缺乏参与意愿的青年尤其有帮助。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C010_16 -->

<!-- WORD:W011 -->
# cultivate

**词源**：来自拉丁语 colere（耕作、照料）一系，经中世纪拉丁语 cultivare 形成。土地需要持续照料，人的能力和关系也可以这样逐渐培养；这是理解几个现代意义的联系。

<!-- USE:U011_01 -->
## cultivate /ˈkʌltəveɪt/ v. · 耕作土地
`准备并利用土地种植作物，可接 land、soil、garden 等宾语；强调实际耕作，不只是拥有土地。cultivated land 是动词的过去分词用法，指已经耕作的土地。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C011_01 USE:U011_01 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/cache/epub/4924/pg4924-images.html"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S011_01_01 USE:U011_01 -->
Before choosing land for dry-farming, learn the annual rainfall of the area you intend to cultivate.
<!-- SENTENCE:S011_01_02 -->
Also examine the soil: you need to know not only its fertility but also its ability to absorb and retain rain and snow.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C011_01 -->
选择土地开展旱作之前，要了解打算耕作地区的年降水量。也要考察土壤：不仅要知道它的肥力，还要了解它吸收和保持雨水、雪水的能力。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C011_01 -->
<!-- USE:U011_02 -->
## cultivate /ˈkʌltəveɪt/ v. · 中耕松土
`用耕作工具翻松土壤、清除杂草，尤其指作物生长期间的中耕；宾语是土壤或地块，不等于每次都重新播种。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C011_02 USE:U011_02 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"","sourceUrls":["https://www.gov.uk/find-funding-for-land-or-farms/cab11-cultivated-areas-for-arable-plants"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S011_02_01 USE:U011_02 -->
For this Countryside Stewardship action, cultivate in spring or autumn, or both, to create a fine soil surface.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C011_02 -->
参加这项乡村环境管理措施时，要在春季或秋季，或这两个季节都进行耕作，形成细碎的土壤表层。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C011_02 -->
<!-- USE:U011_03 -->
## cultivate /ˈkʌltəveɪt/ v. · 栽培作物
`以照料、种植的方式生产植物或作物，直接接 crop、plant 等宾语。与耕地义的区别在于这里关注栽培的植物。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C011_03 USE:U011_03,U011_09 KIND:excerpt -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/files/45917/45917-h/45917-h.htm"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S011_03_01 USE:U011_09 -->
The cultivated lettuce often spreads from gardens, and sows itself in the open country.
<!-- SENTENCE:S011_03_02 USE:U011_03 -->
No one, as far as I know, has observed it in such a case for several generations, or has tried to cultivate the wild L. Scariola, to see whether the transition is easy from the one form to the other.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C011_03 -->
栽培莴苣常会从花园中逸出，在野外自行播种。据我所知，还没有人连续几代观察过这种情况下的莴苣，也没有人尝试栽培野生的 L. Scariola，来看看一种形态是否容易转变成另一种。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C011_03 -->
<!-- USE:U011_04 -->
## cultivate /ˈkʌltəveɪt/ v. · 培养细菌或细胞
`在适宜条件下使细菌、细胞等生长，属于生物培养语境；宾语是所培养的生物材料，不是人的性格。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C011_04 USE:U011_04 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"","sourceUrls":["https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC7875968/"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S011_R04_01 USE:U011_04 -->
Imaging cells can be difficult when they are cultivated on semitransparent supports.
<!-- SENTENCE:S011_R04_02 -->
In this study, we report an optical imaging prototype that produces detailed 3D views without labelling the cells.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C011_04 -->
细胞在半透明载体上培养时，对它们成像可能很困难。在本研究中，我们报告了一种光学成像原型系统，能够无需给细胞作标记就获得细致的三维图像。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C011_04 -->
<!-- USE:U011_05 -->
## cultivate /ˈkʌltəveɪt/ v. · 培养能力或态度
`有意识地投入努力，使技能、思想、态度、习惯或形象发展得更成熟；可接 skill、attitude、image 等，不表示自然就有了某种品质。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C011_05 USE:U011_05 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/files/8554/8554-h/8554-h.htm"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S011_05_01 USE:U011_05 -->
If you really wish to worry less, cultivate some tolerance for things that do not match your ideals, even when this conflicts with a favourite virtue.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C011_05 -->
如果确实想少些忧虑，就要培养一点对不符合自己理想的事物的容忍，即使这样会与你格外看重的一种美德发生冲突。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C011_05 -->
<!-- USE:U011_06 -->
## cultivate /ˈkʌltəveɪt/ v. · 经营友谊或结交某人
`主动花心思建立、维护友好关系，既可接 friendship、relationship，也可直接接人；接有影响力的人时，语境可能带有为了取得好感而结交的意味，不必一概理解为真挚友谊。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C011_06 USE:U011_06 KIND:excerpt -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/files/30373/30373-h/30373-h.htm"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S011_06_01 USE:U011_06 -->
Cultivate your neighbor's friendship; he needs you and you need him.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C011_06 -->
用心经营与邻人的友谊吧；他需要你，你也需要他。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C011_06 -->
<!-- USE:U011_07 -->
## cultivable /ˈkʌltəvəbəl/ adj.; cultivatable /ˈkʌltəveɪtəbəl/ adj. · 可耕种的
`土地、土壤等适合或能够用于耕作；两种词形都表示可耕种，与 cultivated（已经耕作的）不同，能力或条件不等于已经实施。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C011_07 USE:U011_07 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/files/4509/4509-h/4509-h.htm"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S011_07_01 USE:U011_07 -->
In his historical guide Three Acres and Liberty, Bolton Hall describes the Atlantic states of his day as offering two conditions for intensive cultivation: a dense population and idle, cultivable land.
<!-- SENTENCE:S011_07_02 -->
He advises would-be small growers to choose a community where their experience can be put to use.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C011_07 -->
在旧著《三英亩与自由》中，博尔顿·霍尔认为，他那个时代的美国大西洋沿岸各州具备集约耕作的两项条件：稠密的人口，以及闲置但可耕种的土地。他建议有意成为小规模种植者的人选择能够发挥自身经验的社区。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C011_07 -->
<!-- USE:U011_08 -->
## cultivated /ˈkʌltəveɪtɪd/ adj. · 有教养有修养的
`形容人受过良好教育、举止得体、有文化修养，是正式表达；不能仅凭拥有学历就替别人作这种评价。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C011_08 USE:U011_08 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/cache/epub/8399/pg8399-images.html"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S011_08_01 USE:U011_08 -->
At an informal afternoon gathering, we may introduce our guest to authors, artists, lawyers, public-spirited citizens, and cultivated women of society whom we are fortunate enough to know.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C011_08 -->
在一次非正式的午后聚会中，我们可以把来宾介绍给自己有幸认识的作家、艺术家、律师、热心公益的市民，以及有修养的社交界女性。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C011_08 -->
<!-- USE:U011_09 -->
## cultivated /ˈkʌltəveɪtɪd/ adj. · 人工栽培的
`指为农田或花园种植而培育的植物、品种，与 wild 相对；通常置于名词前。并不单指野生植物偶然被人采到。`

<!-- CONTEXT_REF:C011_03 USE:U011_09 -->
<!-- USE:U011_10 -->
## cultivation /ˌkʌltəˈveɪʃən/ n. · 耕种与栽培
`不可数名词，指耕地、种植作物的活动；cultivation of 后接所栽培的作物，under cultivation 表示土地处于耕作利用中。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C011_10 USE:U011_10 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/cache/epub/62715/pg62715-images.html"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S011_10_01 USE:U011_10 -->
Before growing medicinal plants commercially, make sure that the species selected for cultivation suits the local soil and climate.
<!-- SENTENCE:S011_10_02 -->
Where necessary, test it on small experimental plots before starting commercial plantings.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C011_10 -->
商业种植药用植物之前，要确认选定栽培的物种适合当地的土壤和气候。必要时，应先在小型试验地块上试种，再开展商业种植。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C011_10 -->
<!-- USE:U011_11 -->
## cultivation /ˌkʌltəˈveɪʃən/ n. · 生物培养
`不可数名词，指在受控条件下培养细菌、细胞等；cultivation of 指明培养对象，与农作物栽培共享“促其生长”的联系，但对象和操作条件不同。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C011_11 USE:U011_11 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"","sourceUrls":["https://wwwnc.cdc.gov/eid/article/30/1/AC-3001_article"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S011_R11_01 USE:U011_11 -->
Although Koch and Pasteur helped advance germ theory and the cultivation of bacteria in broth and other culture media, some bacteria still grow poorly by conventional methods.
<!-- SENTENCE:S011_R11_02 -->
These demanding organisms have remained a challenge, prompting the use of newer identification techniques, including genomic sequencing.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C011_11 -->
尽管科赫和巴斯德推动了病菌理论的发展，也促进了在营养肉汤等培养基中培养细菌的方法，一些细菌用传统方法仍然难以生长。这些对生长条件要求较高的细菌一直是个难题，因而人们采用了基因组测序等更新的鉴定技术。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C011_11 -->
<!-- USE:U011_12 -->
## cultivation /ˌkʌltəˈveɪʃən/ n. · 品质能力的培养
`不可数名词，指有意识地培养思想、态度、能力或修养；常用 cultivation of，强调培养过程，而不只是已经具有的品质。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C011_12 USE:U011_12 KIND:excerpt -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/files/15490/15490-h/15490-h.htm"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S011_12_01 USE:U011_12 -->
All cultivation requires watchfulness and additional precautions, either more or less: you must not, for the sake of a few superable difficulties, resign the otherwise unattainable refinement effected by poetry.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C011_12 -->
一切培养都多少需要留心和额外防范：不能因为几项可以克服的困难，就放弃诗歌能带来、而用其他方式难以获得的修养。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C011_12 -->
<!-- USE:U011_13 -->
## cultivation /ˌkʌltəˈveɪʃən/ n. · 人际关系的经营
`不可数名词，指主动建立和维系友谊、关系的过程；of 后可接关系，也可接被刻意结交的人，具体动机须看上下文。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C011_13 USE:U011_13 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/files/63377/old/63377-h/63377-h.htm"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S011_R13_01 -->
Henry James liked a communicative companion.
<!-- SENTENCE:S011_R13_02 USE:U011_13 -->
The cultivation of friendships, Theodora Bosanquet suggests, could be called his only recreation: he quickly recognised opportunities to form friendly relations and was ready to nurture the affection that grew.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C011_13 -->
亨利·詹姆斯喜欢能畅谈的同伴。西奥多拉·博桑凯认为，经营友谊可以说是他唯一的消遣：他很快就能发现建立友好关系的机会，并愿意用心培育随之萌生的感情。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C011_13 -->
<!-- USE:U011_14 -->
## cultivator /ˈkʌltəveɪtər/ n. · 中耕工具或耕耘机
`可数名词，指翻松土地、除草的工具或机器；不能把一切农业机器都叫 cultivator。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C011_14 USE:U011_14 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/files/4509/4509-h/4509-h.htm"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S011_14_01 USE:U011_14 -->
Soon after the plants appear, break up the hard soil surface between them with a hand cultivator or hoe.
<!-- SENTENCE:S011_14_02 -->
Keep the surface loose through the season to control weeds and help retain moisture.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C011_14 -->
植株刚露出地面不久，就用手动中耕器或锄头松开植株之间坚硬的土壤表层。在整个生长季保持表层疏松，以控制杂草并帮助保水。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C011_14 -->
<!-- USE:U011_15 -->
## cultivator /ˈkʌltəveɪtər/ n. · 耕种或培育者
`可数名词，指从事耕种、栽培或培养的人，也可按所培养的对象指起培养作用的事物；cultivator of 后交代对象。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C011_15 USE:U011_15 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/files/4509/4509-h/4509-h.htm"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S011_15_01 USE:U011_15 -->
Some cultivators of city lots on Long Island kept a record of the days they worked.
<!-- SENTENCE:S011_15_02 -->
According to the account quoted by Bolton Hall, they earned wages comparable to those of mechanics rather than ordinary farm workers.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C011_15 -->
长岛一些耕种城市地块的人记录了自己工作的天数。按照博尔顿·霍尔所引用的记录，他们所得报酬与技术工人的工资相当，而不是普通农场工人的工资水平。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C011_15 -->
<!-- USE:U011_16 -->
## uncultivated /ʌnˈkʌltəveɪtɪd/ adj. · 未经耕作的
`尤其形容土地没有种植作物、未开发耕种；可以是尚未开垦，也可以是有意留作非耕作区域，不必理解为贫瘠或无法种植。可在名词前或系动词后使用。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C011_16 USE:U011_16 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/cache/epub/4076/pg4076-images.html"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S011_16_01 -->
Ohthere told King Alfred about his voyage along the coast.
<!-- SENTENCE:S011_16_02 USE:U011_16 -->
All the land on his right was uncultivated, with only a few fishermen, fowlers, and hunters living there.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C011_16 -->
奥瑟向阿尔弗雷德国王讲述了他的沿岸航行。右侧的土地全都未经耕作，那里只有少数渔民、捕鸟人和猎人居住。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C011_16 -->
<!-- USE:U011_17 -->
## uncultivated /ʌnˈkʌltəveɪtɪd/ adj. · 缺乏教养或修养的
`形容人的文化教育、举止或审美修养未得到培养；不等于智力低，也不等于品德败坏。可修饰人，也可评价其举止或趣味；这是带评价色彩的说法，不能只凭学历作判断。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C011_17 USE:U011_17 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/files/15419/15419-h/15419-h.htm"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S011_17_01 -->
In her nineteenth-century letters to a daughter, Helen Ekin Starrett urges her reader to value consideration for others above outward elegance.
<!-- SENTENCE:S011_17_02 USE:U011_17 -->
People who lack that consideration remain uncultivated, she argues, even when their surroundings offer every comfort, beauty, and luxury.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C011_17 -->
在十九世纪写给女儿的书信中，海伦·埃金·斯塔雷特劝读者把体谅他人看得比外在的优雅更重要。她认为，缺少这种体谅的人，即使周围尽是舒适、美丽与奢华之物，仍然缺乏修养。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C011_17 -->
<!-- USE:U011_18 -->
## uncultivated /ʌnˈkʌltəveɪtɪd/ adj. · 未经人工照料生长的
`形容植物等未经人工照料而生长、发展，与人工栽培或养护相对；关注生长过程未受照管，不是说它所在的土地一定不能耕种，也不保证是本土物种。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C011_18 USE:U011_18 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/files/37607/37607-h/37607-h.htm"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S011_18_01 -->
In his nineteenth-century book Garden Design and Architects' Gardens, William Robinson insists that a flower garden is a home for cultivated nature.
<!-- SENTENCE:S011_18_02 USE:U011_18 -->
Trees and shrubs need care at first; if we wish to encourage uncultivated nature, he argues, we should do so a little further afield.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C011_18 -->
在十九世纪的著作《园林设计与建筑师的花园》中，威廉·罗宾逊坚持认为，花园是经过人工培育的自然景物的家园。他指出，树木和灌木起初需要照料；如果我们想让未经人工照料的自然生长得到发展，就应当在离花园稍远一些的地方这样做。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C011_18 -->
<!-- USE:U011_19 -->
## uncultivated /ʌnˈkʌltəveɪtɪd/ adj. · 未经训练培养的
`形容才能、心智或某种能力尚未经过训练和努力得到发展；能力可以已经存在，但仍待培养。常见于 uncultivated talent 等搭配，与形容整个人缺乏文化修养的评价不同。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C011_19 USE:U011_19 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/cache/epub/26495/pg26495-images.html"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S011_19_01 USE:U011_19 -->
In A System of Logic, John Stuart Mill follows Dugald Stewart in arguing that even an uncultivated understanding can accept that AB equals CD when both equal EF, once the statements are understood.
<!-- SENTENCE:S011_19_02 -->
The person need never have heard the general axiom that things equal to the same thing are equal to one another.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C011_19 -->
在《逻辑体系》中，约翰·斯图亚特·密尔承接杜格尔德·斯图尔特的观点指出：一旦理解了这些陈述，即使未经系统培养的理解能力，也能接受这样的推断——既然AB和CD都等于EF，那么AB就等于CD。作出推断的人甚至可能从未听说过“等于同一事物的事物彼此相等”这条一般公理。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C011_19 -->

<!-- WORD:W012 -->
# discrepancy

**词源**：来自拉丁语 discrepantia，与 discrepare（声音不同、意见不合）相关；dis- 表分开，后半部与发出响声有关。现代义是应当对应的事物“对不上”。

<!-- USE:U012_01 -->
## discrepancy /dɪˈskrepənsi/ n. · 应当相符却存在的差异
`指两个本应相同、对应或一致的数字、说法、记录等不相符；既可不可数地谈不一致，也可数地指出一处或多处差异，复数 discrepancies。常用 discrepancy between A and B 或 discrepancy in 某记录；普通差别不一定构成这种需要核对的出入。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C012_01 USE:U012_01 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"","sourceUrls":["https://www.gov.uk/hmrc-internal-manuals/trust-registration-service-manual/trsm70070"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S012_01_01 USE:U012_01 -->
When reporting discrepancies to HMRC's Trust Registration Service, Relevant Persons must submit a separate report for each trust.
<!-- SENTENCE:S012_01_02 USE:U012_01 -->
They may include several discrepancies in one report, provided all of them concern the same trust.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C012_01 -->
向英国税务海关总署的信托登记服务报告出入时，负有相应报告义务的相关人士必须为每一个信托分别提交报告。一份报告中可以包含多处出入，前提是这些出入都涉及同一个信托。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C012_01 -->
<!-- USE:U012_02 -->
## discrepant /dɪˈskrepənt/ adj. · 彼此不相符的
`形容记录、结果、说法等存在差异、不能对应；强调不一致这一性质。它不是“令人失望”，也不先行判断究竟哪一项正确。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C012_02 USE:U012_02 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"","sourceUrls":["https://www.gov.uk/government/publications/cystic-fibrosis-screening-laboratory-handbook/cystic-fibrosis-screening-laboratory-handbook"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S012_02_01 USE:U012_02 -->
In NHS newborn screening for cystic fibrosis, a blood-spot card may yield discrepant results.
<!-- SENTENCE:S012_02_02 -->
Assaying further samples from the same card is unlikely to resolve the issue completely.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C012_02 -->
在英国国民医疗服务体系的新生儿囊性纤维化筛查中，一张血斑采样卡可能得出彼此不一致的结果。继续检测同一张卡上的样本，不大可能彻底解决这一问题。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C012_02 -->

<!-- WORD:W013 -->
# dwell

**词源**：来自古英语 dwellan，早期与耽搁、停留有关，后来发展出居住义。dwell on 可借“停留在某事上”理解反复思考或详谈，但现代用法仍须分别看对象与构式。

<!-- USE:U013_01 -->
## dwell on /dwel ɑːn/ v.; dwell upon /dwel əˈpɑːn/ v. · 长久想着或谈论
`花较长时间思考、谈论或书写某个话题，尤其常用于不愉快的事，也可中性地表示详谈；on/upon 后接话题。过去式和过去分词均可用 dwelt 或 dwelled，不等于一闪而过地想到。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C013_01 USE:U013_01 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/cache/epub/16225/pg16225-images.html"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S013_01_01 USE:U013_01 -->
Within a single chapter, I can dwell on only a few practical points about teaching and organising piano work in a school.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C013_01 -->
在一章的篇幅之内，我只能详细谈谈学校钢琴教学与组织安排中的几个实际问题。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C013_01 -->
<!-- USE:U013_02 -->
## dwell /dwel/ v. · 居住在
`不及物动词，后接 in、at、near 等地点表达，表示居住、栖居；偏正式或文学表达，日常通常用 live。过去式、过去分词可为 dwelt 或 dwelled。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C013_02 USE:U013_02 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/cache/epub/4076/pg4076-images.html"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S013_02_01 USE:U013_02 -->
Ohthere told King Alfred that he dwelt on the mainland, to the north of all the other Northmen.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C013_02 -->
奥瑟告诉阿尔弗雷德国王，自己住在大陆上，位置比其他所有诺斯人都更靠北。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C013_02 -->
<!-- USE:U013_03 -->
## dwell /dwel/ v. · 马收步或跃前迟疑
`用于马的动作，指抬蹄缓慢，或跨越障碍前停顿、迟疑；是不及物用法。不能把这个专门对象义泛化为任何人的犹豫。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C013_03 USE:U013_03 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/files/57116/57116-h/57116-h.htm"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S013_R03_01 USE:U013_03 -->
Hayes describes horses that dwell at fences: they clear the obstacle but cannot get away quickly after landing.
<!-- SENTENCE:S013_R03_02 -->
He links this slow movement to training that puts too much weight on the forehand.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C013_03 -->
海斯描述过一种在障碍处收步迟缓的马：它们跳得过障碍，却不能在落地后迅速离开。他把这种迟缓与一种使过多重量压在前躯上的训练方式联系起来。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C013_03 -->
<!-- USE:U013_04 -->
## dwell /dwel/ n. · 机器运转的规律停顿
`指机器运动周期中短暂、规律的停顿，属于机械语境；可说 a dwell 或 dwell time，讨论的是运动安排，并非故障后停止工作。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C013_04 USE:U013_04 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/cache/epub/50532/pg50532-images.html"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S013_R04_01 USE:U013_04 -->
In Nelson's account of Jacquard looms, cams gave the opening between warp threads a dwell while the shuttle passed through.
<!-- SENTENCE:S013_R04_02 -->
But the sharp rise and fall could break weights and cords, so he preferred the smoother double crank for general use.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C013_04 -->
在尼尔森对提花织机的说明中，凸轮让经纱间的开口在梭子通过时停留片刻。但急剧的升降可能使吊锤和绳索断裂，因此他认为，一般使用时，运动更平顺的双曲柄机构更好。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C013_04 -->
<!-- USE:U013_05 -->
## dwell on /dwel ɑːn/ v.; dwell upon /dwel əˈpɑːn/ v. · 拉长声音或音节
`在某个音、音节上停留得较久，使其延长；on/upon 后接 note、syllable 等声音单位。与反复思考的话题义区别在于这里延长的是发声时间。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C013_05 USE:U013_05 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/files/47382/old/47382-h/47382-h.htm"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S013_R05_01 -->
In Rippmann’s exercise, the aim is to keep the vocal cords vibrating evenly.
<!-- SENTENCE:S013_R05_02 USE:U013_05 -->
Dwell on different voiced sounds, letting the breath out slowly and regularly.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C013_05 -->
在里普曼设计的练习中，目标是让声带保持均匀振动。把不同浊音拉长，同时缓慢、均匀地呼气。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C013_05 -->
<!-- USE:U013_06 -->
## dweller /ˈdwelər/ n. · 某地居民
`可数名词，通常与地点限定语结合，指居住在该类地方的人，如 city dweller、slum dweller；并不表示此人拥有所住的房屋。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C013_06 USE:U013_06 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/cache/epub/17804/pg17804-images.html"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S013_R06_01 -->
In a country house supplied by a tank and pump, taps left running can empty even a large tank.
<!-- SENTENCE:S013_R06_02 -->
Until the pump refills it, nobody has water.
<!-- SENTENCE:S013_R06_03 USE:U013_06 -->
“Waste not, want not” is a useful motto for dwellers in the country.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C013_06 -->
在靠水箱和水泵供水的乡间住宅里，水龙头如果一直开着，连大水箱也会被放空。在水泵重新补足供水之前，谁都没有水可用。“不浪费，才不匮乏”是乡村居民的一条实用格言。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C013_06 -->
<!-- USE:U013_07 -->
## dwelling /ˈdwelɪŋ/ n.; dwelling place /ˈdwelɪŋ pleɪs/ n. · 住处住所
`可数名词，正式地指供人居住的地方、房屋或寓所；可用于住房规划中的住宅单位。这里是名词，区别于 dwell 的现在分词。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C013_07 USE:U013_07 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"","sourceUrls":["https://www.ons.gov.uk/peoplepopulationandcommunity/housing/articles/ukhousebuildingdata/latest"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S013_07_01 USE:U013_07 -->
This release combines published data from England, Wales, Scotland and Northern Ireland to provide quarterly UK totals for newly built dwellings.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C013_07 -->
这份统计发布汇集了英格兰、威尔士、苏格兰和北爱尔兰已公布的数据，提供全英国新建住宅数量的季度总数。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C013_07 -->
<!-- USE:U013_08 -->
## dwelling house /ˈdwelɪŋ haʊs/ n. · 用作居所的房屋
`以居住为用途的住宅，区别于办公室等非居住用途的房屋；是名词短语，不把 dwelling 单独标成形容词。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C013_08 USE:U013_08 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"","sourceUrls":["https://www.gov.uk/guidance/when-is-permission-required"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S013_08_01 USE:U013_08 -->
In England, agricultural buildings may be converted into dwelling houses under Class Q, subject to the conditions and restrictions in the permitted-development rules.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C013_08 -->
在英格兰，农业建筑可以依照Q类规定改建为住宅，但须符合许可开发规则所规定的条件和限制。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C013_08 -->
<!-- USE:U013_09 -->
## cave dweller /ˈkeɪv ˌdwelər/ n. · 穴居人
`可数名词，指居住在洞穴中的人，与 caveman 的穴居人义相对应；谈史前人类时要根据具体时代与证据理解，不是所有史前人类都住在洞穴里。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C013_09 USE:U013_09 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/files/24065/24065-h/24065-h.htm"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S013_09_01 USE:U013_09 -->
In his 1899 book A History of Nursery Rhymes, Percy B. Green praises the cave-dweller’s remarkable talent for depicting the animals he hunted.
<!-- SENTENCE:S013_09_02 -->
He sees in the sketches an eye for the beauty and grace of natural objects.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C013_09 -->
格林在1899年的著作《童谣史》中，赞赏穴居人描绘自己捕猎的动物的非凡才能。他从这些素描中看到了对自然物体之美与优雅的欣赏能力。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C013_09 -->
<!-- USE:U013_10 -->
## indwell /ɪnˈdwel/ v. · 精神或原则内在常存
`指精神、原则等长久存在于某人或某物之中，可不及物并接 in，也可及物直接接所存在于的对象，表示从精神层面居于其中。过去式和过去分词 indwelt；不是普通搬进住宅。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C013_10 USE:U013_10 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/cache/epub/6038/pg6038-images.html"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S013_R10_01 USE:U013_10 -->
William Evans teaches that the Spirit indwells every believer, however weak or inexperienced.
<!-- SENTENCE:S013_R10_02 -->
He distinguishes this abiding presence from being filled with the Spirit, which he says may happen repeatedly.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C013_10 -->
威廉·埃文斯教导说，圣灵常驻每一位信徒内心，即使信徒软弱或信仰经验尚浅也一样。他把这种持续的内居，与他说可以反复发生的“被圣灵充满”区别开来。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C013_10 -->
<!-- USE:U013_11 -->
## indweller /ɪnˈdwelər/ n. · 内在居存者
`可数名词，指在内部居存的主体；随 indwell 的精神、原则等对象理解，不能仅因含 dweller 就当作日常“住户”的一般替换。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C013_11 USE:U013_11 KIND:excerpt -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/cache/epub/21190/pg21190-images.html"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S013_11_01 USE:U013_11 -->
Holding fellowship with the divine Indweller and giving the reins into His strong hand, we receive from Him the spirit of adoption and learn that if we are children then are we heirs.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C013_11 -->
我们与内居的神圣者保持相交，把缰绳交到祂有力的手中，便从祂领受儿女身份的心，并认识到：既是儿女，也就是继承人。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C013_11 -->
<!-- USE:U013_12 -->
## indwelling /ˈɪnˌdwelɪŋ/ adj. · 长期置留体内的
`医学用法，形容导管、针等放置于体内并留置一段较长时间；常置于器械名词前。它说明放置方式，不自动等于永久不取出。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C013_12 USE:U013_12 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"","sourceUrls":["https://www.cdc.gov/uti/hcp/clinical-overview/"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S013_12_01 USE:U013_12 -->
People using indwelling urinary catheters, whether for a short or a long period, have an increased risk of catheter-associated urinary tract infections.
<!-- SENTENCE:S013_12_02 -->
A catheter can introduce bacteria directly into the bladder.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C013_12 -->
使用留置导尿管的人，无论留置时间较短还是较长，发生导管相关尿路感染的风险都会增加。导管可能把细菌直接带入膀胱。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C013_12 -->
<!-- USE:U013_13 -->
## lake-dwelling /ˈleɪk ˌdwelɪŋ/ n. · 史前湖上桩屋
`可数名词，指史前建于湖底或湖岸木桩上的小屋；不是所有现代湖景住宅。这里 dwelling 为名词，lake 限定其类型。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C013_13 USE:U013_13 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/files/30610/30610-h/30610-h.htm"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S013_13_01 USE:U013_13 -->
In History of Human Society, Blackmar describes lake dwellings built out over the water, reached by boat or by a narrow walkway that could be removed when not in use.
<!-- SENTENCE:S013_13_02 -->
This helped their inhabitants defend themselves against attack.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C013_13 -->
布莱克马在《人类社会史》中描述了建在水面之上的湖上桩屋：可以乘船到达，也可以通过不用时能移走的狭窄通道进入。这有助于居民防御袭击。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C013_13 -->
<!-- USE:U013_14 -->
## lake-dweller /ˈleɪk ˌdwelər/ n. · 湖上桩屋居民
`可数名词，指居住在湖上桩屋中的人；对象随 lake-dwelling 的史前桩屋语境理解，不泛指所有住在湖区的人。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C013_14 USE:U013_14 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/files/65966/65966-h/65966-h.htm"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S013_14_01 USE:U013_14 -->
Writing in 1887, John Bell Bouton points readers interested in the prehistoric lake-dwellers of Switzerland towards Zürich.
<!-- SENTENCE:S013_14_02 -->
Wooden piles that once supported their villages have been found along the shore of Lake Zürich.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C013_14 -->
约翰·贝尔·布顿在1887年的旅行记述中，向对瑞士史前湖上桩屋居民感兴趣的读者介绍苏黎世一带。苏黎世湖岸发现了曾经支撑他们村落的木桩。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C013_14 -->
<!-- USE:U013_15 -->
## pile dwelling /ˈpaɪl ˌdwelɪŋ/ n. · 桩架住宅
`可数名词，指由桩柱支撑的住屋，尤其是湖中的住屋；pile 在这里是支撑桩，并非“一堆东西”。它不以史前时代为必需条件。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C013_15 USE:U013_15 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/files/46924/46924-h/46924-h.htm"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S013_15_01 USE:U013_15 -->
In his 1922 book The Bronze Age and the Celtic World, Harold Peake reports finds of pile-dwellings across the mountain region, from Geneva and Neuchâtel in Switzerland to Laibach on the edge of the Hungarian plain.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C013_15 -->
哈罗德·皮克在1922年的著作《青铜时代与凯尔特世界》中记述，从瑞士的日内瓦、纳沙泰尔，一直到匈牙利平原边缘的莱巴赫，整个山地区域都发现过桩架住宅的遗存。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C013_15 -->
<!-- USE:U013_16 -->
## indwelling /ˈɪnˌdwelɪŋ/ adj. · 内居而起引导作用的
`形容在内部长久存在、起激发或引导作用的精神、力量等；宗教论述中可说 indwelling Spirit。它与医疗器械留置义不同，也不只是普通租住在某处。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C013_16 USE:U013_16 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/cache/epub/21190/pg21190-images.html"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S013_16_01 USE:U013_16 -->
For Maclaren, the Christian life is one in which an indwelling Christ casts out the old self and thereby gives the person life.
<!-- SENTENCE:S013_16_02 -->
Christ's presence does not destroy individuality but heightens it.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C013_16 -->
在麦克拉伦看来，基督徒的生命是这样的：内居心中的基督驱走旧我，由此赋予人生命。基督的同在并不摧毁人的个性，反而使之更加鲜明。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C013_16 -->
<!-- USE:U013_17 -->
## cave dweller /ˈkeɪv ˌdwelər/ n. · 城市公寓楼居民
`也可比喻居住在城市公寓楼里的人；这是城市住宅语境中的比喻称呼，并非真的住在洞穴，也不能仅凭此称呼推断生活粗野。使用时须有城市住房语境使比喻清楚。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C013_17 USE:U013_17 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"","sourceUrls":["https://www.usmodernist.org/PCA/PCA-21-22.pdf"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S013_R17_01 -->
In his 1922 review of San Francisco apartments, Harris Allen notes the appeal of turning a living room into a bedroom with a wall bed and dressing closet.
<!-- SENTENCE:S013_R17_02 USE:U013_17 -->
For the cave dweller, he argues, this saves time, space and labour.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C013_17 -->
在1922年一篇评论旧金山公寓的文章中，哈里斯·艾伦谈到用壁床和更衣间把客厅改作卧室的便利。他认为，对城市公寓中的“穴居者”来说，这样可以节省时间、空间和劳动。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C013_17 -->

<!-- WORD:W014 -->
# elaborate

**词源**：来自拉丁语 elaborare（费力做出、精心完成），与 labor（劳动）相关。可借“花工夫做细”联系形容词的精巧繁复与动词的详加发展；形容词结尾 /ət/，动词结尾 /eɪt/。

<!-- USE:U014_01 -->
## elaborate /ɪˈlæbərət/ adj. · 复杂而精细的
`形容事物由许多部分构成、发展得复杂精巧，如项目、仪式；通常放在名词前。复杂性本身不一定是褒义，也不必意味着没有实用价值。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C014_01 USE:U014_01 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/files/51559/old/51559-h/51559-h.htm"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S014_01_01 -->
When a pilot is killed, we feel we ought to attend the funeral.
<!-- SENTENCE:S014_01_02 USE:U014_01 -->
The ceremony is usually elaborate; at the last one, the captain broke down in tears during his eulogy.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C014_01 -->
一名飞行员牺牲后，我们觉得应该参加葬礼。仪式通常很隆重繁复；最近那次，队长在致悼词时悲痛得哭了起来。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C014_01 -->
<!-- USE:U014_02 -->
## elaborate /ɪˈlæbərət/ adj. · 细致策划的
`形容计划、体系、程序等经过极细致的安排，有时暗示细节过多；常置于名词前。重点是精心展开的安排，而非单纯规模大。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C014_02 USE:U014_02 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/cache/epub/32856/pg32856-images.html"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S014_R02_01 USE:U014_02 -->
Müller illustrates the range of Leibniz’s projects with an elaborate plan for an expedition to Egypt.
<!-- SENTENCE:S014_R02_02 -->
Leibniz submitted it to Louis XIV in an attempt to divert his attention from Germany’s frontiers.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C014_02 -->
缪勒用一项精心制订的埃及远征计划，说明莱布尼茨涉猎项目之广。莱布尼茨把计划呈给路易十四，意在把他的注意力从德国边境引开。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C014_02 -->
<!-- USE:U014_03 -->
## elaborate /ɪˈlæbərət/ adj. · 装饰精工繁复的
`形容服饰、材料、图案等含有大量细致的艺术设计；不等于贵重或只因颜色多而复杂，通常置于名词前。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C014_03 USE:U014_03 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/cache/epub/70049/pg70049-images.html"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S014_03_01 USE:U014_03 -->
In his 1895 account of Egyptian decorative art, Petrie describes chequered patterns becoming less common during the imperial period as more elaborate decoration developed.
<!-- SENTENCE:S014_03_02 -->
One pleasing variation lengthened the squares, probably following patterns used in weaving.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C014_03 -->
皮特里在1895年对埃及装饰艺术的论述中指出，帝国时期随着装饰发展得更加繁复，棋盘格图案变得较少见了。一种颇美观的变化是把方格拉长，这可能是仿照织物图案形成的。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C014_03 -->
<!-- USE:U014_04 -->
## elaborate /ɪˈlæbəreɪt/ v. · 发展完善计划理论
`及物动词，把计划、政策、理论等详细展开、发展得更完善，直接接对象；可以是设计和形成细节，不只是把现成内容再说一遍。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C014_04 USE:U014_04 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/cache/epub/20233/pg20233-images.html"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S014_04_01 -->
Auguste Comte undertook to explain human intellectual and moral life through natural laws.
<!-- SENTENCE:S014_04_02 USE:U014_04 -->
He elaborated that project in his Cours de Philosophie Positive.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C014_04 -->
奥古斯特·孔德试图用自然规律解释人类的思想生活和道德生活。他在《实证哲学教程》中对这一构想作了详细发展。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C014_04 -->
<!-- USE:U014_05 -->
## elaborate /ɪˈlæbəreɪt/ v.; elaborate on /ɪˈlæbəreɪt ɑːn/ v. · 进一步详细说明
`把已经提到的事讲得更详细；可直接接所解释内容，也可用 elaborate on 后接话题，或在内容已知时单独用 elaborate。提问 Would you care to elaborate? 是请对方多说一些，不是请其把话说得复杂。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C014_05 USE:U014_05 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"","sourceUrls":["https://hansard.parliament.uk/Commons/2022-06-21/debates/3e5a3d2b-c639-4689-9b15-cf28ee8f69c0/OnlineSafetyBill(ThirteenthSitting)"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S014_R05_01 USE:U014_05 -->
During a 2022 debate on online safety, Maria Miller asked the Minister to elaborate on how Ofcom would make people aware of misleading information online.
<!-- SENTENCE:S014_R05_02 -->
She wanted to know whether Ofcom must publicise its reports; Chris Philp replied that it was required to publish them and to promote media literacy.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C014_05 -->
在2022年一次有关网络安全的议会辩论中，玛丽亚·米勒请部长进一步说明，英国通信管理局将如何让人们意识到网上存在的误导性信息。她想知道，该机构是否必须向公众宣传其报告；克里斯·菲尔普回答说，该机构有义务公布报告，并促进公众的媒介素养。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C014_05 -->
<!-- USE:U014_06 -->
## elaborate /ɪˈlæbəreɪt/ v. · 费工制作出
`及物动词，指经过劳动或精心制作产生某物，宾语是成果；强调做出的过程，区别于只解释它。此义不要求成果一定装饰繁复。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C014_06 USE:U014_06 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/cache/epub/63382/pg63382-images.html"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S014_R06_01 USE:U014_06 -->
Discussing Zola’s little sketches of death, Edmund Gosse finds most of them inferior to the studies of the same kind that Tolstoy’s genius has elaborated.
<!-- SENTENCE:S014_R06_02 -->
Only the sketch of Madame Rousseau, the stationer’s wife, seems to him among the best.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C014_06 -->
谈到左拉描写死亡的几篇小品时，埃德蒙·戈斯认为，其中大多数不及托尔斯泰凭其才华精心创作的同类作品；只有描写文具店老板妻子卢梭夫人的那篇，在他看来属上乘之作。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C014_06 -->
<!-- USE:U014_07 -->
## elaborate /ɪˈlæbəreɪt/ v. · 自然过程合成物质
`以自然作用或生物体为主体，从成分、元素或原料形成物质；及物用法，宾语是生成物。不是科学家对物质“详细解说”，也不等于向物质添加无关细节。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C014_07 USE:U014_07 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/cache/epub/24931/pg24931-images.html"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S014_R07_01 USE:U014_07 -->
In his 1860 account of plant growth, Thomas Anderson describes simple compounds absorbed from air and soil being elaborated within the plant into the complex substances of its tissues.
<!-- SENTENCE:S014_R07_02 -->
To make these changes easier to understand, he begins with germination, when the young plant lives on food stored in the seed.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C014_07 -->
托马斯·安德森在1860年的植物生长论述中，描述植物从空气和土壤吸收的简单化合物，如何在植物体内经自然作用形成组织中的复杂物质。为便于读者理解这些变化，他从萌发讲起：幼株此时依靠种子内贮存的养分生长。
<!-- END_CONTEXT:C014_07 -->
<!-- USE:U014_08 -->
## elaborately /ɪˈlæbərətli/ adv. · 极其细致地
`修饰策划、组织、制作或论证等动作，强调处理得细密详尽；语境可褒可贬，不自动表示有效。elaborately planned 指经过细密策划，详加论证时也可用此副词；装饰艺术的繁复另看下一用法。`

<!-- CONTEXT:C014_08 USE:U014_08 KIND:abridgment -->
<!-- CONTEXT_META:{"title":"","sourceUrls":["https://www.gutenberg.org/files/57877/57877-h/57877-h.htm"]} -->
<!-- SENTENCE:S014_08_01 -->
According to Swift's history of postal agitation, the Postmaster-General's denial provoked further criticism.
<!-- SENTENCE:S014_08_02 USE:U014_08 -->
Fresh evidence then elaborately proved, in the writer's view, that Arnold Morley had been too hasty in denying the facts.
<!-- TRANSLATION:C014_08 -->
